refinery maintenance schedules to avoid simultaneous shutdowns and increase utilization rates, it said, advocating for engagement with non G7-countries that hold additional capacity.
The statement followed an announcement from US President Donald Trump on Truth Social declaring that Europe has agreed to immediately begin releasing a "massive amount" of its "heavily stocked diesel," after reports of White House requests for leading EU countries to release extra reserves, or potentially face US export bans.
ICE Low Sulfur Gasoil futures -- a metric for European distillates values -- plummeted on the initial statement from Trump, but have since corrected following the G7 statement. Values fell 8% from the previous close to $1,331/mt, before rebounding to $1,364/mt by 1600 GMT.
The IEA, which counts all the G7 nations among its 32 member states, committed to releasing a historic 400 million barrels of oil to the market in March, but had so far only recorded 300 million barrels of deliveries, according to data published in August. Of the supplies released to the market, 110 million barrels came from the Americas, compared with 112 million barrels from Asia-Oceania and 68 million barrels from Europe, the IEA reported in August.
After weeks of US discussions of a potential diesel export ban, the G7 additionally stressed its commitment to "refrain from export restrictions on energy and energy products" between member states, and said it would call on oil producers to avoid imposing bans.
"We welcome the decision of G7 countries not to impose any export bans on allies and the continued solidarity between partners," said European Commission President Ursula von Der Leyen in a statement on the social media platform X. "We support an IEA coordinated release of fuel stocks," she said. A full export ban could have deprived Europe of the source of half its recent diesel imports, according to EU figures, potentially pushing up prices already hovering at record highs.
The G7 recommended that a follow-up report should be delivered within 20 days of its statement, including recommendations for future responses and stock replenishment plans.
US lobby According to Politico, US Energy Secretary Chris Wright had
requested that European countries release 120 million barrels of

oil over a three-month period, with France, which holds the G7 presidency, reported to have suggested a new 100 million barrel stock release, split between crude and oil products.
The IEA has said the 400 million barrels of oil it committed to release in March represents just 20% of the stocks its members have at their disposal, although deliveries have slowed to a trickle in recent months. In the EU, diesel stockpiles have slumped to five-year lows, although backwardated market structures have encouraged sales to come mostly from commercial stocks. Meanwhile, the US Strategic Petroleum Reserve, already at 44year lows, has neared its operational floor.
EU officials have maintained that diesel supplies remain stable in the region, but have warned of a "tough winter" ahead as prices continue to surge. After an informal EU ministers' meeting on Sept. 29, the bloc's energy commissioner, Dan Jørgensen, said officials were concerned with "finding a balance" on stock releases to preserve energy security for even more challenging conditions.
"A staggered release could tide Europe through winter, when heating demand typically pressures stocks," said Eleanor Budds, an oil research and analysis director at S&P Global Energy CERA, who warned that dampening effects for prices could still prove short-lived. "It is a sticking plaster that does not resolve the underlying problem of global tightness, with no clear visibility on when Middle Eastern and Russian volumes will return to the market," said Eleanor Budds, an oil research and analysis director at S&P Global Energy CERA.

Mongolia diversifies fuel imports to mitigate supply risks
South Korea, China ship total of 10,000 mt diesel
Country holds just 11-day fuel reserve
New refinery reaches 60% completion mark
Mongolia has diversified its imports of fuel in order to mitigate supply risks, arising from the situation in Russia, the Ministry of Industry and Mineral Resources said in a statement.
The country has received the first shipments of 2,000 metric tons of diesel from South Korea and 8,000 mt from China.
"As diesel fuel is essential for the continuous operation of key economic sectors such as mining and agriculture, efforts are being intensified to accelerate procurement and transport in order to meet rising demand during the autumn harvest season and ensure a stable supply," the ministry said in a statement posted this week.
In early September, the country's Prime Minister Nyam-Osoryn Uchral said at the Eastern Economic Forum in Vladivostok that Mongolia's current annual consumption amounts to around 3 million metric tons and 90% of it is imported from Russia. He also said that the attacks on Russian refineries "are having a very negative impact on our country," but added that, irrespective of its difficult situation, Russia continues to supply fuel to Mongolia.
In the statement, the Ministry of Industry also said that measures are being implemented to maintain a steady supply of 92 RON gasoline from Russia.
It also said that the country holds an 11-day reserve of petroleum products.
In order to mitigate long-term fuel supply risks, Mongolia is diversifying import sources while simultaneously implementing a phased expansion of domestic fuel storage capacity, according to the statement.
It also added that the construction of the country's first oil refinery has been accelerated and the project has reached over 60% completion. It reiterated that the refinery will become operational in 2028.
Mongolia is on track to start its 1.5 million mt/year Dornogovi refinery in 2028, Platts has reported previously.
Separately, Mongolia has contracted deliveries of oil products in October from Russia, China, South Korea, and Singapore, Russia's TASS news agency reported, citing the ministry.

Japex said it acquired Fundare Resources Operating Co. LLC, Fundare Redtail LLC, Rangeview Resources Operating Co. LLC and Rangeview Green River LLC, which hold tight oil and gas assets in Colorado and Wyoming.
Of the $278 million purchase price, about $220 million will be raised through reserve-based lending, with the remainder covered by the internal funds of Peoria, an overseas subsidiary of Japex.
Japex said it decided on Aug. 6 to enter into an agreement to acquire four US tight oil and gas producers for $320 million, positioning the US as its key strategic base for early earnings generation and capability enhancement.
Japex said Aug. 6 that its acquisition of the equity interests in Fundare will enable it to conduct development more efficiently and reduce costs by expanding its business scale. Fundare's net production averaged about 9,500 barrels of oil equivalent/day over January-March 2026.
The move followed Japex's announcement on Feb. 27 that it had completed its purchase of US tight oil and gas assets by acquiring Verdad Resources Intermediate Holdings for $1.26 billion.
Under the transaction completed on Feb. 26 local time, Japex acquired a 100% stake in Verdad Resources Intermediate Holdings, which produces about 35,000 boe/d of tight oil and gas in the US through Peoria Resources Acquisition Co.
In its April 22 midterm business plan, Japex said it aims to quadruple its equity oil and gas production to 180,000 boe/d by the end of fiscal year 2035-36 (April-March), citing output growth in the US, Norway and Southeast Asia.
In its plan starting fiscal year 2026-27, Japex said it has positioned its exploration and production business as a core pillar, aiming to more than double equity oil and gas production to 100,000 boe/d in FY 2031-32 and quadruple it from an estimated 45,000 boe/d in fiscal year 2025-26.
-- Takeo Kumagai
OIL FUTURES: Oil slides as G7 says it will release 100 million barrels of reserves ...from page 1
"It's still a short-term fix for a long-term problem that is getting a long tail on it," Bannockburn Capital Markets Managing Director, Commodities, Darrell Fletcher told Platts, part of S&P Global Energy.
"Price action was steep to begin but is already fading," Fletcher said. "For now it may be another sell the rumor and buy this back prior to a weekend of more unknowns."
NYMEX November RBOB settled down 9.02 cents/gallon at $3.3124/gal, and November ULSD declined 14.09 cents/gal to $4.5011/gal.
The White House and Congressional Republicans have mulled a potential limitation on US diesel exports in recent weeks, sending jitters through US and European diesel markets. Trump said as recently as Sept. 30 that potential export limitations were

still under discussion, but analysts said that the G7 release could reduce the likelihood of such action.
"We cannot wholly rule out export constraints governing destinations outside the G7, but odds now look lower," Clearview Energy Partners analysts said in an Oct. 2 note.
Other federal actions to improve diesel supply, such as easing restrictions on dyed fuel that has been designated only for offroad use, may be imminent, the analysts added.
"The White House might be contemplating a federal waiver of dyed diesel limitations either in place of, or in addition to, export restrictions," the analysts said. "It is not clear what authority the Trump Administration might invoke for this purpose."
At least eight US states -- Alabama, Indiana, Louisiana, Nebraska, Minnesota, Oklahoma and Texas -- have already reduced restrictions in whole or in part on the use of dyed fuels.
-- Christopher Vanmoessner
Refinery updates
REFINERY NEWS: Ukrainian military hits Russia's Volgograd
Refinery: Volgograd, Russia
Owner: Lukoil
Overall capacity: 15.7 million mt/year (314,000 b/d)
The Ukrainian military has carried out a strike on Russia's Volgograd refinery, resulting in a fire at the site, it said in a Telegram post Oct. 2.
According to the Ukrainian Security Service, satellite imagery detected hotspots near the coker unit and the primary processing complexes ELOU-AVT-1 and ELOU-AVT-5.
Volgograd was not selling products on the St. Petersburg exchange Oct. 2, according to Russia-based market sources.
Volgograd has been targeted previously and was temporarily halted, although it has recently been operating at reduced capacity, according to Russia-based market sources.
The Ukrainian military also said it had hit a crude oil pumping station at Samara, causing a fire.
The Samara pumping station is a major delivery point for several pipelines shipping crude, where it is blended into the Urals export grade. The pumping station also supplies crude to the nearby Samara refining hub.
Separately, Russia's Deputy Prime Minister Alexander Novak said at the Federation Council on Oct. 2, as cited by local media, that Ukrainian drones had attacked four Russian refineries overnight, but all attacks had been repelled and no equipment damaged. At one plant, damage was being assessed.
Novak added that refinery defense systems had been strengthened and that damage sustained has been much less significant recently. Novak noted that as a result of the better protection, oil companies are bringing back online damaged equipment faster after attacks.

October 2, 2026

Novak also noted that the government is considering extending the timeline for refineries to complete modernizations as currently it is more important to repair damaged refineries and equipment.
Russian refineries are currently implementing the second phase of their government-mandated modernization program, although the timelines have been pushed back since 2022 due to the impact of Western sanctions on technology.
-- Elza Turner
REFINERY NEWS: Bilbao gets EU funds for preflash tower
Refinery: Petroleos del Norte SA, Bilbao, Spain
Owner: 86% Repsol SA, 14% Kutxabank SA
Overall capacity: 11 million mt/yr, or 220,000 b/d
The Bilbao refinery operator Petroleos del Norte SA, or Petronor, said in a note dated Oct. 2 that it received 2 million of European Union funding from the NextGenerationEU fund for its new Pre-flash Tower in crude unit 1.
Crude unit 1 is the smaller of two at Bilbao, with 100,000 barrels/day capacity.
Total investment in the preflash tower is expected at 9.7 million, Petronor said, leading to a CO2 emission reduction of 27,630.62 mt of CO2/year. It did not say what the work entailed. Repsol was contacted for comment Oct. 2.
The project falls under the incentive program for selfconsumption and storage using renewable energy sources, as well as the implementation of renewable thermal systems.
Bilbao said in March 2025 it installed a new preflash tower in C1 to separate light ends such as butane and gasoline from middle distillates without heating them in a furnace.
The unit enables the refinery to process around 8% of the volume that is usually fed into crude unit 1 and heated to 360 degrees C, it said, allowing a reduction of fuel consumption and improving efficiency of the atmospheric distillation unit.
-- Gianluca Baratti

Platts proposes amending freight calculation for Oman, Murban CFR North Asia crude assessments Nov. 2
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, is proposing to amend the freight calculation used in its daily Oman and Murban CFR North Asia crude assessments, effective Nov. 2, 2026.
Under the proposed change, Platts would use the daily VLCC Gulf of Oman-to-Far East freight assessment in $/mt (DOOEW00), which was launched Aug. 3, in its calculation of the outright price of Oman and Murban delivered into North Asia at 4:30 pm Singapore time (0830 GMT).
The proposed change would better reflect the shipping economics for the two grades, with Murban primarily loading from Fujairah port and Oman from Mina Al Fahal port outside the Strait of Hormuz.
Currently, the freight calculation is based on the daily VLCC Persian Gulf-to-Far East freight assessment in $/mt (TDAAB00).
The freight calculation would continue to use the conversion factor for metric tons to barrels, as applied to the grades, in line with their prevailing qualities. The conversion factors are currently 7.32 for Oman and 7.62 for Murban.
In line with this change, Platts is also proposing to use the daily VLCC Gulf of Oman-to-Far East worldscale freight assessment (DIOEW00) in the freight calculation for Murban in its Indonesian Minas, Cinta and Widuri crude market parity prices.
Under the current MPP methodology for Minas, Cinta and Widuri, Murban -- as one of five basket grades -- is normalized for delivery to Singapore, accounting for freight. This is currently calculated using the daily VLCC Persian Gulf-to-Singapore worldscale freight assessment (PFAOD00).
The affected assessments appear in Crude Oil Marketwire, Oilgram Price Report, LNG Daily, Platts OPR World Crude Table, Platts Global Alert/Platts Crude Oil Alert pages 1250, 1805, 2202, 2203, 2211, 2216, 2217, 2244, 2245, 2264, 2265, 2280, 2281, 2811 and 2812, LNG Alert pages 0050 and 0051, Platts Natural Gas Alert pages 1024 and 1069, and under the price database codes below:

Assessments Oman CFR North Asia Murban CFR North Asia Minas Singapore close Minas London close Cinta Singapore close Cinta London close Widuri Singapore close Widuri London close Minas FOB Indonesia $/MMBtu

Daily PCAQJ00 PCAQE00 PCABO00 AAPAZ00 PCAAX00 AAPBJ00 PCAFE00 AAPBN00 LCABO00

Platts proposes to amend rollover date of Asian Dated Brent Strip at London close
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, is proposing to amend the rollover date of its Asian Dated Brent Strip at London close

assessment to the first publication day of the month, from Dec. 1, 2026.
The proposed change, which follows Platts review of historical data sets, would align the ADB Strip at London close assessment with the current rollover date of its ADB Strip at Asia close assessment.
Currently, this assessment, which reflects the forward value of Dated Brent two months ahead of the date of publication at the 4:30 pm London close, rolls over on the 9th of the month.
The assessments affected by this change appear in Crude Oil Marketwire, Asia Pacific/Arab Gulf Marketscan, Oilgram Price Report, Platts Global Alert/ Platts Crude Oil Alert pages 2206, 2213, 2215, 2217, 2219, 2260, 2261, 2262, 2263, 2264, 2265, 2266, 2267, 2268, 2269, 2270, 2271, 2280, 2281, 2558, 2559, and under their price database codes as below:
